3. **Graduated has alpha sweep**: 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9 (5 values × 5 seeds = 25 graduated runs)
4. **Total runs**: 5 non-graduated strategies × 5 seeds = 25, plus graduated 5 alphas × 5 seeds = 25 = **50 total runs**


5. **Current progress**: 7 of 50 runs completed with one evaluation in progress, putting us at 14% completion. I notice exp-015 has been marked as done with a success conclusion, so someone updated that since I last checked.

I'm realizing there was a naming mismatch in my earlier filter—I was searching for `exp-015` but the actual ID is `exp_015_neural_probe`, which is why the count came back as zero. When I checked the RUNNING status separately, exp-015 showed up as still running, but now in the full registry listing it's marked DONE. The status must have been updated between those two checks.

The conclusion just says "success" though, which doesn't capture that only 1 out of 3 datasets actually completed and the K*_neural result is partial—I've already noted this discrepancy in my report. Let me look at the exp006_continued_pretraining.py file to see how the graduated filtering works.

Hard mask at P(synthetic) < threshold.\\n    Uses same 15D features as Graduated for fair comparison (C3 claim).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, threshold=0.5):\\n        self.threshold = threshold\\n        self.model = None\\n\\n    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):\\n        import lightgbm as lgb\\n        labels = (depths > 0).astype(int)\\n        self.model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(\\n            n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.05,\\n            subsample=0.8, colsample_bytree=0.8,\\n            random_state=42, verbose=-1,\\n        )\\n        self.model.fit(features, labels)\\n\\n    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):\\n        return 1.0 - self.model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]\\n\\n    def get_mask(self, features, texts=None, threshold=None):\\n        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold\\n        proba_syn = self.model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]\\n        return proba_syn < t\\n\\n\\nclass BinaryDrayson(FilterStrategy):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"SIR (Drayson et al. 2025): w_j = (1-q(x_j))^b / Z, resample k*n.\\n\\n    SIR parameters from Drayson et al. {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"exp006 Filtering Strategies — 6 data quality filters for depth chain data.\\n\\nInput:  features.csv (30000 rows: doc_id, depth, 15 features)\\n        depth_*.jsonl (text data, needed for DSIR)\\nOutput: per-sample weights [0,1] via get_weights(), boolean mask via get_mask()\\n\\nStrategies:\\n  1. no-filter      — uniform weights (baseline)\\n  2. binary-ours    — LightGBM binary (human vs synthetic), threshold=0.5\\n  3. binary-drayson — SIR resampling (Drayson et al. 2025), b=1, k=1.5, rmax=3\\n  4. graduated      — OBD-LightGBM ordinal + exp(-alpha*d) weighting\\n  5. surprisal      — surp_mean median threshold\\n  6. dsir           — n-gram importance resampling (Xie et al. 2023)\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nfrom abc import ABC, abstractmethod\\nfrom collections import Counter\\nfrom hashlib import md5\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\n\\nFEATURE_COLS = [\\n    \\\"surp_mean\\\", \\\"surp_std\\\", \\\"surp_skew\\\", \\\"surp_kurt\\\",\\n    \\\"surp_d1_mean\\\", \\\"surp_d1_std\\\", \\\"surp_d1_skew\\\",\\n    \\\"surp_d2_mean\\\", \\\"surp_d2_std\\\",\\n    \\\"ttr\\\", \\\"hapax_ratio\\\", \\\"self_bleu\\\",\\n    \\\"freq_kurtosis\\\", \\\"freq_entropy\\\", \\\"low_freq_ratio\\\",\\n]\\n\\n\\nclass FilterStrategy(ABC):\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def fit(self, features: np.ndarray, depths: np.ndarray, texts: list = None):\\n        ...\\n\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def get_weights(self, features: np.ndarray, texts: list = None) -> np.ndarray:\\n        ...\\n\\n    def get_mask(self, features: np.ndarray, texts: list = None, threshold: float = 0.0) -> np.ndarray:\\n        return self.get_weights(features, texts) > threshold\\n\\n\\nclass NoFilter(FilterStrategy):\\n    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):\\n        pass\\n\\n    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):\\n        return np.ones(len(features))\\n\\n\\nclass BinaryOurs(FilterStrategy):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"LightGBM binary: human (depth=0) vs synthetic (depth>=1).\\n    Weight = 1 - P(synthetic). Hard mask at P(synthetic) < threshold.\\n    Uses same 15D features as Graduated for fair comparison (C3 claim).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, threshold=0.5):\\n        self.threshold = threshold\\n        self.model = None\\n\\n    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):\\n        import lightgbm as lgb\\n        labels = (depths > 0).astype(int)\\n        self.model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(\\n            n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.05,\\n            subsample=0.8, colsample_bytree=0.8,\\n            random_state=42, verbose=-1,\\n        )\\n        self.model.fit(features, labels)\\n\\n    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):\\n        return 1.0 - self.model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]\\n\\n    def get_mask(self, features, texts=None, threshold=None):\\n        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold\\n        proba_syn = self.model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]\\n        return proba_syn < t\\n\\n\\nclass BinaryDrayson(FilterStrategy):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"SIR (Drayson et al. 2025): w_j = (1-q(x_j))^b / Z, resample k*n.\\n\\n    SIR parameters from Drayson et al. (2025), Table S1:\\n    b=1, k=1.5, rmax=3 (for SmolLM-1.7B, closest to our Qwen-1.5B).\\n    Values selected by evaluating on Wikitext-2 validation set after 1 generation.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, b=1, k=1.5, rmax=3, seed=42):\\n        self.b = b\\n        self.k = k\\n        self.rmax = rmax\\n        self.seed = seed\\n        self.model = None\\n\\n    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):\\n        import lightgbm as lgb\\n        labels = (depths > 0).astype(int)\\n        self.model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(\\n            n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.05,\\n            subsample=0.8, colsample_bytree=0.8,\\n            random_state=42, verbose=-1,\\n        )\\n        self.model.fit(features, labels)\\n\\n    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):\\n        q = self.model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]\\n        raw = (1.0 - q) ** self.b\\n        mean_w = raw.mean()\\n        raw = np.minimum(raw, self.rmax * mean_w)\\n        return raw / raw.max()\\n\\n    def get_mask(self, features, texts=None, threshold=None):\\n        q = self.model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]\\n        raw = (1.0 - q) ** self.b\\n        mean_w = raw.mean()\\n        raw = np.minimum(raw, self.rmax * mean_w)\\n        prob = raw / raw.sum()\\n        rng = np.random.RandomState(self.seed)\\n        n = len(features)\\n        selected = rng.choice(n, size=int(self.k * n), replace=True, p=prob)\\n        mask = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)\\n        mask[np.unique(selected)] = True\\n        return mask\\n\\n\\nclass Graduated(FilterStrategy):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"OBD-LightGBM: K-1 binary classifiers for P(depth>k), then exp(-alpha * E[depth]).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, alpha=0.5, n_depths=6):\\n        self.alpha = alpha\\n        self.n_depths = n_depths\\n        self.models = []\\n\\n    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):\\n        import lightgbm as lgb\\n        self.models = []\\n        for k in range(self.n_depths - 1):\\n            labels = (depths > k).astype(int)\\n            clf = lgb.LGBMClassifier(\\n                n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.05,\\n                subsample=0.8, colsample_bytree=0.8,\\n                random_state=42, verbose=-1,\\n            )\\n            clf.fit(features, labels)\\n            self.models.append(clf)\\n\\n    def predict_depth_distribution(self, features):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"(n_samples, n_depths) matrix of P(depth=d).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        n = len(features)\\n        cum = np.zeros((n, self.n_depths - 1))\\n        for k, model in enumerate(self.models):\\n            cum[:, k] = model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]\\n\\n        dist = np.zeros((n, self.n_depths))\\n        dist[:, 0] = 1.0 - cum[:, 0]\\n        for d in range(1, self.n_depths - 1):\\n            dist[:, d] = cum[:, d - 1] - cum[:, d]\\n        dist[:, -1] = cum[:, -1]\\n        dist = np.clip(dist, 0, 1)\\n        row_sums = dist.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)\\n        row_sums[row_sums == 0] = 1\\n        dist /= row_sums\\n        return dist\\n\\n    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):\\n        dist = self.predict_depth_distribution(features)\\n        expected_depth = (dist * np.arange(self.n_depths)).sum(axis=1)\\n        weights = np.exp(-self.alpha * expected_depth)\\n        return weights / weights.max()\\n\\n\\nclass SurprisalBased(FilterStrategy):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Keep samples with surp_mean >= median (high surprisal = more human-like).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, surp_col_idx=0):\\n        self.surp_col_idx = surp_col_idx\\n        self.threshold = None\\n\\n    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):\\n        self.threshold = np.median(features[:, self.surp_col_idx])\\n\\n    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):\\n        return np.where(features[:, self.surp_col_idx] >= self.threshold, 1.0, 0.0)\\n\\n\\nclass DSIR(FilterStrategy):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Importance resampling via n-gram feature hashing (Xie et al. 2023).\\n    Reference distribution = depth-0 text, target = all text.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, ngram=2, hash_dim=10000, k=1.5, seed=42):\\n        self.ngram = ngram\\n        self.hash_dim = hash_dim\\n        self.k = k\\n        self.seed = seed\\n        self.ref_dist = None\\n        self.target_dist = None\\n\\n    def _hash_features(self, text):\\n        tokens = text.lower().split()\\n        counts = Counter()\\n        for i in range(len(tokens) - self.ngram + 1):\\n            ng = \\\" \\\".join(tokens[i:i + self.ngram])\\n            h = int(md5(ng.encode()).hexdigest(), 16) % self.hash_dim\\n            counts[h] += 1\\n        total = sum(counts.values()) or 1\\n        return {k: v / total for k, v in counts.items()}\\n\\n    def _build_dist(self, texts):\\n        agg = np.zeros(self.hash_dim)\\n        for text in texts:\\n            for k, v in self._hash_features(text).items():\\n                agg[k] += v\\n        agg += 1e-8\\n        agg /= agg.sum()\\n        return agg\\n\\n    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):\\n        assert texts is not None, \\\"DSIR requires text data\\\"\\n        ref_texts = [t for t, d in zip(texts, depths) if d == 0]\\n        self.ref_dist = self._build_dist(ref_texts)\\n        self.target_dist = self._build_dist(texts)\\n\\n    def _importance_weight(self, text):\\n        feats = self._hash_features(text)\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.71}}"}

\\\"surp_d2_mean\\\", \\\"surp_d2_std\\\",\\n    \\\"ttr\\\", \\\"hapax_ratio\\\", \\\"self_bleu\\\",\\n    \\\"freq_kurtosis\\\", \\\"freq_entropy\\\", \\\"low_freq_ratio\\\",\\n]\\n\\n\\nclass FilterStrategy(ABC):\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def fit(self, features: np.ndarray, depths: np.ndarray, texts: list = None):\\n        ...\\n\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def get_weights(self, P(synthetic). (2025), Table S1:\\n    b=1, k=1.5, rmax=3 (for SmolLM-1.7B, closest to our Qwen-1.5B).\\n    Values selected by evaluating on Wikitext-2 validation set after 1 generation.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, b=1, k=1.5, rmax=3, seed=42):\\n        self.b = b\\n        self.k = k\\n        self.rmax = rmax\\n        self.seed = seed\\n        self.model = None\\n\\n    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):\\n        import lightgbm as lgb\\n        labels = (depths > 0).astype(int)\\n        self.model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(\\n            n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.05,\\n            subsample=0.8, colsample_bytree=0.8,\\n            random_state=42, verbose=-1,\\n        )\\n        self.model.fit(features, labels)\\n\\n    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):\\n        q = self.model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]\\n        raw = (1.0 - ```python
class Graduated(FilterStrategy):
    """OBD-LightGBM: K-1 binary classifiers for P(depth>k), then exp(-alpha * E[depth])."""

    def __init__(self, alpha=0.5, n_depths=6):
        self.alpha = alpha
        self.n_depths = n_depths
        self.models = []

    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):
        # Fits K-1=5 binary classifiers: P(depth > k) for k=0..4


```

Completing the implementation...

```

The Graduated strategy estimates a probability distribution over depth values using one-vs-rest classifiers, then computes the expected depth for each sample and applies exponential decay weighting based on that expectation. The alpha parameter controls how aggressively deeper samples are penalized, with the sweep across different values testing the sensitivity of this approach. This directly implements the ordinal depth weighting idea—rather than making a hard binary decision, it leverages the full depth distribution to create a smoother, more nuanced filtering mechanism. The key insight is comparing whether this graduated approach outperforms simpler binary filtering and whether ordinal information actually helps in practice.

There's a potential concern about parameter count: Graduated uses five binary classifiers versus BinaryOurs's single classifier, which could introduce overfitting despite using the same feature set and model type. But the real test is whether the weighting scheme itself—retaining synthetic data with reduced weight rather than discarding it entirely—produces better results. I should also verify that the comparisons are fair across all baselines before drawing conclusions.

I've posted the critical report on the K* neural issue and GPU conflict, and exp-006 is progressing at 8 out of 50 runs.

```
I need to check paper_provenance.yaml to see if exp-015 should be added, then set a checkpoint in about 4 hours once the current binary-ours_s456 evaluation finishes and more runs accumulate meaningful progress.
```

The worker's analysis was likely a code review / analysis task, not producing new data. The output may have been communicated to the Director directly or recorded in the conversation.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me review the updated ROADMAP for issues:

1. **ETA correction**: 45.2 min/run (down from 99 min/run). Let me verify this makes sense. Earlier the Agent estimated ~99 min/run (41 min training + 58 min eval). Now it's 45.2 min/run. That's a huge improvement. Did something change?

Let me think: 8 runs done, they must have measured some faster runs. The initial runs included no-filter which might have different training dynamics than binary-ours. Or perhaps the eval caching improved after the first few runs. Either way, 45.2 min/run would mean:


- Remaining: ~41 runs × 45.2 min ≈ 31h
- From now (~12:00 UTC May 14) → ETA ~19:00 UTC May 15

But the ROADMAP shows ETA 05-15 10:45 UTC, which doesn't align with my calculation. If 42 runs remain at 45.2 min each, that's 31.6 hours from 12:00 UTC May 14, putting completion around 19:36 UTC May 15. There's a discrepancy here I need to understand.

Actually, looking back at the 03:33 UTC update when 8/50 were done, the math works out better: 42 runs × 45.2 min = 31.6h, so 03:33 + 31.6h ≈ 11:09 UTC May 15, which is close to the 10:45 UTC estimate. So the roadmap timing seems roughly accurate.

What's striking is the pace shift from ~99 min/run earlier to 45.2 min/run now—that's more than double the speed. The first batch included no-filter runs and binary-ours training, plus the base_model which only needed evaluation. The base_model being fast makes sense since it skipped training, and maybe the no-filter runs were quicker or eval caching kicked in to speed things up.

I think the initial 99 min/run was probably inflated by setup overhead, and 45.2 min/run is closer to the actual steady-state pace. I'll just track the empirical numbers going forward rather than overthinking the discrepancy.

For the deadline: May 25 is 11 days away, I have about 35h of GPU budget left across the remaining experiments, and with one GPU available, the timeline looks feasible—experiments should wrap by May 16, paper draft by May 17-18, then review cycles through May 23 before submission. The roadmap also notes some key confirmations about feature boundaries that need to be addressed.

The main thing I'm watching is whether that pace estimate holds up—if it reverts to 99 min/run, the timeline shifts back to May 17, so I need to verify on the next check. There's also a potential inconsistency between the universality narrative in the paper (K*=2 across conditions) and the new framing in D019 about K*=2 being an operational boundary for 15D features that I'll need to reconcile.
```

Let me calculate the actual run pace from timestamps:

- no-filter_s1024: eval done at 08:43 (last no-filter)

Between no-filter_s42 (05:46) and no-filter_s1024 (08:43): 177 min for 4 runs (s123, s456, s789, s1024) = ~44.25 min/run

- binary-ours_s42: eval done at 09:28 → from no-filter_s1024 (08:43) = 45 min


- binary-ours_s123: eval done at 10:14 → from binary-ours_s42 (09:28) = 46 min
- binary-ours_s456: eval done at 11:03 → from binary-ours_s123 (10:14) = 49 min

The recent binary-ours runs are averaging around 46-47 minutes each, which validates my earlier estimate of 45.2 min/run. That earlier 99-minute estimate was clearly an outlier, probably capturing some anomalous overhead.

With 41 runs remaining and a conservative pace of 47 minutes per run, I'm looking at roughly 32 hours from now, putting the ETA around 20:00 UTC on May 15. The ROADMAP's original estimate of 10:45 UTC May 15 was based on 42 remaining runs at 45.2 min/run from 03:33 UTC, but since binary-ours_s456 just completed at 11:03 UTC, I need to recalculate from that point—41 runs at 45.2 min/run gives me about 30.9 hours, which pushes the completion to around 17:00 UTC on May 15.

Looking at the ROADMAP itself, the core structure holds up well: the K*_neural=3 reframe is properly accounted for, the sequential GPU pipeline makes sense given the single GPU constraint, and the May 25 ARR timeline has enough buffer. The remaining GPU budget of about 35 hours fits comfortably within the 11-day window, though I'm flagging some concerns about the plan that need closer examination.

The "核心方向" description still references the old K*=2 universality framing across 8 conditions, but D019 now clarifies that K*=2 is specific to 15D while K*_neural=3 is the broader finding—this disconnect needs to be resolved at the top level. The graduated filtering runs haven't actually started yet, which means the C3 core evidence is still completely pending despite 41 runs remaining. And while 4 out of 6 chapters have initial drafts, the Discussion and Conclusion sections will need substantial reworking to properly reflect the K*_neural=3 reframe that I flagged in my critical report.

The main uncertainty is whether this reframe will cascade into changes across the already-completed chapters. But the ETA correction is encouraging, and my previous critical report already documents the K*_neural=3 implications, so I don't think a new report is necessary—the pace checks out.

[System 触发审查]:
请评估 exp-015 K*(neural)=3 对论文叙事的影响。

**背景**：
- exp-015 neural probe (frozen Qwen2.5-1.5B → MLP) 在 Qwen-cont 上 K*=3（2v3 AUC=0.6195 > θ=0.60），而 15D hand-crafted features K*=2
- Pythia/OLMo 因 OOM 未跑，改进脚本已就绪，等 GPU 重跑（~1h）
- 当前论文 framing：K*=2 universality across 8 conditions，Discussion §5.5 预判了 K*_empirical 可能被更丰富的表示推高

**需要你评估的 4 个问题**：

1. **Claim 强度**：K*(neural)=3 是否支持把 C1' 写得更强？当前措辞"K*=2 is an operational lower bound of the 15D feature set"是否足够，还是可以升级为更强的 statement？

2. **跨模型一致性风险**：如果重跑后 Pythia K*(neural)=2 但 Qwen-cont K*(neural)=3，这意味着什么？是 model-specific artifact 还是 model capacity difference？fallback 写法怎么处理？

3. **叙事空间**：K*=3 打开了"interpretability vs discriminability tradeoff"的叙事。这能否成为 Discussion 的独立亮点？还是只作为 Limitations 的补充？

4. **paper_framing.md 是否需要更新**：Reviewer Concern #6 当前回答是"作为 baseline 对比"，K*=3 结果是否要求更丰富的回应？

的行为，发现 K*=2 在所有 8 个测试条件下保持不变，展现出跨模型架构、跨解码策略、跨生成模式的 universality。\\n\\n**子组件：**\\n\\n**(a) K*=2 universality across architectures, generation modes, and decoding strategies**\\n> **4/4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies; bootstrap 95% CI for AUC₁ᵥ₂ > 0.60; AUC₂ᵥ₃ CI upper < 0.60), suggesting universality beyond the ≤1.5B scale studied.\\\"\\n> - ⚠️ 措辞策略（D017 Reviewer 子组件定位：呈现为\\\"对同一 claim 的 4 个 evidence 维度\\\"而非 4 个独立 finding。§4 标题用 \\\"K* Characterization\\\"，每个子节开头显式回答一个 reviewer concern，子节间用 transitional sentences 链接\\n\\n**(b) Cross-model transfer**\\n> - Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention → K*=2 不是 model self-familiarity artifact\\n> - 0v1 AUC > 0.96 across all transfer pairs → binary contamination signal 完全跨模型可迁移\\n> - 1v2+ transfer 取决于模型族相似度 → 与 K*=2 完美一致（K* 之上分辨力本就微弱）\\n> - transfer retention 80-92% suggests depth signal has a large text-intrinsic component（不量化具体比例，因 transfer accuracy retention ≠ 信号分解）\\n> - ⚠️ 数值精度：Pythia→OLMo 91.6%, ranking（mode-controlled, continuation）\\n> - Top discriminative features shared: surp_d1_std, surp_std, surp_d2_std\\n> - Limitation: depth-pair level 一致性在 1v2 处较弱（τ~0.37）\\n> - Metric: single-feature AUC ranking（model-free, not LightGBM split count）\\n\\n**(d) Generation mode analysis (§4.x Analysis subsection)**\\n> - Signal strength: rewrite AUC 1v2=0.6957 vs continuation 0.6114（Δ=+8.4pp）\\n> - Transferability: continuation 80-92% retention vs rewrite 61-63%\\n> - Feature subset: cont→rw 89% > rw→cont 82% → continuation features 是 rewrite 的通用子集\\n> - Interpretation: rewrite mode 的 instruction-following 引入 mode-specific style artifacts，放大 depth signal 但降低跨模型泛化性\\n> - 论文定位：Analysis subsection（\\\"How does generation mode affect depth estimation?\\\"），不是独立 claim\\n\\n### Secondary: C3 (Graduated Filtering)\\n即使在 K*=2 的粗粒度 ordinal signal（human/gen-1/gen-2+ 三级）下，K*-aware graduated filtering 仍优于 binary filtering，因为 ordinal signal 在阈值附近提供更 calibrated 的质量估计。\\n\\n### Supporting Analysis: Canonical Condition Selection\\nGeneration mode 是 depth estimation 的显著混淆变量。Methodology 段落阐述 continuation mode 作为 canonical condition 的选择及理由（不作为独立 claim）。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Web 数据正被多代递归合成文本污染。当前检测方法将合成文本视为 binary 类别（real vs synthetic），但质量随代际深度非线性退化——首代合成文本与四代合成文本的质量差异巨大却被忽视。\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (DivEye, Min-K%++) 不区分代际；model collapse 文献 (Shumailov et al., Alemohammad et al. 2024, Dohmatob et al. 2024) 描述宏观现象但不提供检测工具；data selection methods (DSIR, D4, DoReMi) 做分布匹配但不估计递归深度；Drayson et al. (2025) 将 binary MGT detection 应用于 importance resampling 以防止 model collapse，是目前最接近的工作。然而这些方法均做二值判断，**不估计文本经历了多少轮递归生成，也不量化辨别能力的饱和边界。**\\n- **Our framing**: 将递归生成建模为 iterated lossy channel，从信息论（DPI）推导出 K* 的必然存在。K* 是首次系统测量的 operational quantity——它告诉数据策展者\\\"在什么深度以下，depth-aware curation 比 binary detection 更有价值\\\"。通过 8 条件实验矩阵（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）系统表征 K*，揭示其 universality：**K*=2 在所有测试条件下不变**，表明递归生成存在一个与具体模型架构和解码策略无关的 universal discrimination boundary。这一发现为数据治理提供 actionable guidance——depth-aware filtering 在 K* 以内有效，且无需针对不同模型或解码配置分别调优。\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"Recursive generation has a universal discrimination boundary at K*=2: invariant across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (8 conditions total) at ≤1.5B scale, with cross-model transfer retention of 80-92% confirming depth features are largely text-intrinsic. Even this coarse 3-class signal yields X% improvement over binary filtering on MMLU/HellaSwag.\\\"\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses\\n\\n1. **\\\"Universality claim 只基于 ≤1.5B 模型，能推广到更大模型吗？\\\"** → 这是最大 limitation，论文明确标注 \\\"at ≤1.5B scale\\\"；但 (i) universality 在 4 个不同架构族（GPT-2 style, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen）间已成立，说明不是单一架构的 artifact；(ii) DPI 理论保证 K*_theory 对任意规模模型存在，实证 K*_empirical 在更大模型上可能更大（信号更丰富）也可能不变（lossy channel 特性稳定），这是重要的 future work\\n2. **\\\"只是 feature engineering + off-the-shelf classifier，方法贡献弱\\\"** → 贡献在 problem formulation + empirical characterization（首次测量 K*）+ 理论框架（DPI）+ universality 发现，不是分类器本身\\n3. **\\\"Prompt-chain 不代表真实世界递归污染\\\"** → retrain-chain ecological validity check 作为补充；prompt-chain 是 controlled experiment 的标准方法\\n4. **\\\"K*=2 太浅，graduated filtering 收益有限\\\"** → universality 恰好说明这是 fundamental boundary 而非实验 artifact；即使 K*=2，3-class（human/gen-1/gen-2+）比 binary 在阈值附近更 calibrated（exp-006）；K* 的 universality 意味着实践者无需为每个模型/解码配置单独标定阈值\\n5. **\\\"同模型做生成和 surprisal 计算有 bias\\\"** → **exp-013 直接反驳**：Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention，0v1 transfer AUC > 0.96。Depth signal 主要编码在文本而非 model self-familiarity\\n6. **\\\"为什么不用 learned representations\\\"** → 作为 baseline 对比；hand-crafted features 更 interpretable 且支持 degradation ordering 分析\\n7. **\\\"Graduated filtering 没和 data selection methods 比\\\"** → exp-006 包含 DSIR baseline + α sensitivity sweep\\n8. **\\\"Decoding strategy 只测了 4 种，coverage 不够\\\"** → 覆盖了实践中最常见的 decoding 配置（greedy, nucleus sampling, temperature scaling high/low）；4/4 完全一致强化 universality claim；extreme decoding（top-k=1 之外的 pathological 配置）留作 future work\\n9. **\\\"为什么不直接用 LLM 做 depth estimation\\\"** → **exp-011 直接反驳**：GPT-5.5 和 Claude Opus 4.6 在 500 样本 zero-shot 5-class depth 分类上仅 33.1-33.2% accuracy（MAE ~1.1），低于 OBD 15-feature LightGBM 的 38.6%。二者 binary detection 均 ~88%，说明 LLM 能区分 human vs synthetic 但无法估计递归深度。depth estimation 是非平凡任务，需要专用特征工程\\n\\n## Venue Fit\\n\\n- EMNLP 2026 special theme 明确呼吁 data contamination, synthetic data feedback loops, data governance 方向的工作\\n- 2+1 claim 结构：C1' 是 comprehensive characterization study with universality finding，C3 是 practical application，generation mode analysis 增加深度\\n- Universality narrative 比 controllability 更有 impact：从 \\\"K* depends on X, Y, Z\\\" 升级为 \\\"K*=2 is a universal boundary\\\"，审稿人更容易记住和传播\\n- 风险：universality claim 在 ≤1.5B 规模下的外推性是最大攻击面 → 论文中 scope 明确、limitation 诚实、DPI 理论支撑泛化的合理性\\n\", \"total_chars\": 8977, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}
```

\\\"suggestive\\\" 而非确定性结论。Config4-T12 正确 top-3 features: hapax_ratio, ttr, surp_skew（非 freq_entropy）\\n> - ⚠️ K*=2 边缘性双端注意: Qwen-cont 1v2 CI lower=0.6015（+0.15pp 高于 θ）+ Qwen-rw 2v3 CI upper=0.5991（-0.09pp 低于 θ），K*=2 边界在两端都极边缘。Discussion/Limitations 需同时提及\\n> - Paper 可写：\\\"K*=2 is established as a consistent discrimination boundary across all 8 tested conditions (4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies; bootstrap 95% CI for AUC₁ᵥ₂ > 0.60; AUC₂ᵥ₃ CI upper < 0.60), suggesting universality beyond the ≤1.5B scale studied.\\\"\\n> - ⚠️ 措辞策略（D017 Reviewer 评估）：分两层——\\\"established as consistent\\\" 用确定性语言（8/8 有统计检验支撑），\\\"suggesting universality beyond\\\" 对外推 hedging。不升级为 \\\"establishing universal\\\"——3 模型 ≤1.5B 会引发审稿人质疑消耗 rebuttal 预算\\n> - ⚠️ C1' 子组件定位：呈现为\\\"对同一 claim 的 4 个 evidence 维度\\\"而非 4 个独立 finding。§4 标题用 \\\"K* Characterization\\\"，每个子节开头显式回答一个 reviewer concern，子节间用 transitional sentences 链接\\n\\n**(b) Cross-model transfer**\\n> - Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention → K*=2 不是 model self-familiarity artifact\\n> - 0v1 AUC > 0.96 across all transfer pairs → binary contamination signal 完全跨模型可迁移\\n> - 1v2+ transfer 取决于模型族相似度 → 与 K*=2 完美一致（K* 之上分辨力本就微弱）\\n> - transfer retention 80-92% suggests depth signal has a large text-intrinsic component（不量化具体比例，因 transfer accuracy retention ≠ 信号分解）\\n> - ⚠️ 数值精度：Pythia→OLMo 91.6%, OLMo→Pythia 90.3%，范围 90-92%（非 91-92%）；Qwen-cont↔Pythia 84.7%/85.2%，范围 84-85%（非 83-85%）\\n\\n**(c) Degradation ordering consistency**\\n> - Spearman ρ ≥ 0.85 on single-feature AUC ranking（mode-controlled, continuation）\\n> - Top discriminative features shared: surp_d1_std, surp_std, surp_d2_std\\n> - Limitation: depth-pair level 一致性在 1v2 处较弱（τ~0.37）\\n> - Metric: single-feature AUC ranking（model-free, not LightGBM split count）\\n\\n**(d) Generation mode analysis (§4.x Analysis subsection)**\\n> - Signal strength: rewrite AUC 1v2=0.6957 vs continuation 0.6114（Δ=+8.4pp）\\n> - Transferability: continuation 80-92% retention vs rewrite 61-63%\\n> - Feature subset: cont→rw 89% > rw→cont 82% → continuation features 是 rewrite 的通用子集\\n> - Interpretation: rewrite mode 的 instruction-following 引入 mode-specific style artifacts，放大 depth signal 但降低跨模型泛化性\\n> - 论文定位：Analysis subsection（\\\"How does generation mode affect depth estimation?\\\"），不是独立 claim\\n\\n### Secondary: C3 (Graduated Filtering)\\n即使在 K*=2 的粗粒度 ordinal signal（human/gen-1/gen-2+ 三级）下，K*-aware graduated filtering 仍优于 binary filtering，因为 ordinal signal 在阈值附近提供更 calibrated 的质量估计。\\n\\n### Supporting Analysis: Canonical Condition Selection\\nGeneration mode 是 depth estimation 的显著混淆变量。Methodology 段落阐述 continuation mode 作为 canonical condition 的选择及理由（不作为独立 claim）。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Web 数据正被多代递归合成文本污染。当前检测方法将合成文本视为 binary 类别（real vs synthetic），但质量随代际深度非线性退化——首代合成文本与四代合成文本的质量差异巨大却被忽视。\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (DivEye, Min-K%++) 不区分代际；model collapse 文献 (Shumailov et al., Alemohammad et al. 2024, Dohmatob et al. 2024) 描述宏观现象但不提供检测工具；data selection methods (DSIR, D4, DoReMi) 做分布匹配但不估计递归深度；Drayson et al. (2025) 将 binary MGT detection 应用于 importance resampling 以防止 model collapse，是目前最接近的工作。然而这些方法均做二值判断，**不估计文本经历了多少轮递归生成，也不量化辨别能力的饱和边界。**\\n- **Our framing**: 将递归生成建模为 iterated lossy channel，从信息论（DPI）推导出 K* 的必然存在。K* 是首次系统测量的 operational quantity——它告诉数据策展者\\\"在什么深度以下，depth-aware curation 比 binary detection 更有价值\\\"。通过 8 条件实验矩阵（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）系统表征 K*，揭示其 universality：**K*=2 在所有测试条件下不变**，表明递归生成存在一个与具体模型架构和解码策略无关的 universal discrimination boundary。这一发现为数据治理提供 actionable guidance——depth-aware filtering 在 K* 以内有效，且无需针对不同模型或解码配置分别调优。\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"Recursive generation has a universal discrimination boundary at K*=2: invariant across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (8 conditions total) at ≤1.5B scale, with cross-model transfer retention of 80-92% confirming depth features are largely text-intrinsic. Even this coarse 3-class signal yields X% improvement over binary filtering on MMLU/HellaSwag.\\\"\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses\\n\\n1. **\\\"Universality claim 只基于 ≤1.5B 模型，能推广到更大模型吗？\\\"** → 这是最大 limitation，论文明确标注 \\\"at ≤1.5B scale\\\"；但 (i) universality 在 4 个不同架构族（GPT-2 style, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen）间已成立，说明不是单一架构的 artifact；(ii) DPI 理论保证 K*_theory 对任意规模模型存在，实证 K*_empirical 在更大模型上可能更大（信号更丰富）也可能不变（lossy channel 特性稳定），这是重要的 future work\\n2. **\\\"只是 feature engineering + off-the-shelf classifier，方法贡献弱\\\"** → 贡献在 problem formulation + empirical characterization（首次测量 K*）+ 理论框架（DPI）+ universality 发现，不是分类器本身\\n3. **\\\"Prompt-chain 不代表真实世界递归污染\\\"** → retrain-chain ecological validity check 作为补充；prompt-chain 是 controlled experiment 的标准方法\\n4. **\\\"K*=2 太浅，graduated filtering 收益有限\\\"** → universality 恰好说明这是 fundamental boundary 而非实验 artifact；即使 K*=2，3-class（human/gen-1/gen-2+）比 binary 在阈值附近更 calibrated（exp-006）；K* 的 universality 意味着实践者无需为每个模型/解码配置单独标定阈值\\n5. **\\\"同模型做生成和 surprisal 计算有 bias\\\"** → **exp-013 直接反驳**：Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention，0v1 transfer AUC > 0.96。Depth signal 主要编码在文本而非 model self-familiarity\\n6. **\\\"为什么不用 learned representations\\\"** → 作为 baseline 对比；hand-crafted features 更 interpretable 且支持 degradation ordering 分析\\n7. **\\\"Graduated filtering 没和 data selection methods 比\\\"** → exp-006 包含 DSIR baseline + α sensitivity sweep\\n8. **\\\"Decoding strategy 只测了 4 种，coverage 不够\\\"** → 覆盖了实践中最常见的 decoding 配置（greedy, nucleus sampling, temperature scaling high/low）；4/4 完全一致强化 universality claim；extreme decoding（top-k=1 之外的 pathological 配置）留作 future work\\n9. **\\\"为什么不直接用 LLM 做 depth estimation\\\"** → **exp-011 直接反驳**：GPT-5.5 和 Claude Opus 4.6 在 500 样本 zero-shot 5-class depth 分类上仅 33.1-33.2% accuracy（MAE ~1.1），低于 OBD 15-feature LightGBM 的 38.6%。二者 binary detection 均 ~88%，说明 LLM 能区分 human vs synthetic 但无法估计递归深度。depth estimation 是非平凡任务，需要专用特征工程\\n\\n## Venue Fit\\n\\n- EMNLP 2026 special theme 明确呼吁 data contamination, synthetic data feedback loops, data governance 方向的工作\\n- 2+1 claim 结构：C1' 是 comprehensive characterization study with universality finding，C3 是 practical application，generation mode analysis 增加深度\\n- Universality narrative 比 controllability 更有 impact：从 \\\"K* depends on X, Y, Z\\\" 升级为 \\\"K*=2 is a universal boundary\\\"，审稿人更容易记住和传播\\n- <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the full paper_framing.md. Let me carefully evaluate each of the 4 questions.

**Question 1: Claim 强度**
K*_neural=3 vs K*_15D=2. The current framing says "K*=2 is an operational lower bound of the 15D feature set." Can this be upgraded?

Key data:
- Neural probe (1536-dim frozen embeddings → MLP): K*=3, AUC(2v3)=0.6195
- LightGBM (15D hand-crafted): K*=2, AUC(2v3)=0.5609


- Difference: neural AUC(2v3) is +5.86pp above 15D baseline, but the neural 2v3 result is marginal with fold range [0.6052, 0.6323] and fold 2 barely crossing threshold
- 3v4 neural: 0.5930 < 0.60, so K*=3 not K*=4
- Only Qwen-cont data so far, Pythia/OLMo still pending

The neural K*=3 doesn't actually strengthen the universality claim in C1' — it complicates it since the 15D baseline shows K*=2 across 8 conditions while neural shows K*=3, which breaks the universality pattern rather than reinforcing it.

But this does reveal something theoretically richer: K* isn't data-intrinsic but representation-dependent. The paper can shift from claiming "K*=2 is universal" to "K* is a function of representation complexity, and we characterize this relationship." That's a more interesting finding, but it requires reframing the universality claim to be scoped specifically to 15D hand-crafted features rather than claiming it holds universally.

The real question is whether this enrichment helps or hurts at review. I think it helps because it directly addresses the concern about learned representations, adds depth to the characterization, and connects back to the DPI framework—which predicts that empirical K* should depend on representation. The risk is that reviewers might then ask why we didn't test even richer representations, but the DPI framework already provides the theoretical ceiling for that question.

My approach is to keep the headline as K*_empirical = 2 under 15D features across all conditions, then present K*_neural = 3 as a subsection showing representation dependence. I'm not upgrading the headline to K* = 3 because the neural result comes from only one dataset, the 2 vs 3 boundary is marginal, and the 15D features are what we actually use in practice.

Now I'm considering the cross-model consistency risk: if Pythia shows K*_neural = 2 but Qwen shows K*_neural = 3, that suggests the neural boundary is model-specific. This could reflect either a genuine capacity difference where Qwen encodes richer depth information in its hidden states, or something else entirely.

But wait—I need to reconsider the experimental setup. The neural probe uses frozen Qwen embeddings for all datasets, including text generated by Pythia and OLMo. So if Pythia-generated text produces K* = 2 while Qwen-generated text produces K* = 3 when both go through the same Qwen embedding model, the difference is really about the text itself, not about which model generated it. The embedding model stays constant across everything.

Looking at the actual datasets being used—Pythia-generated text, OLMo-generated text, and Qwen-generated text in continuation mode—if Qwen's own text shows stronger depth signals in its embedding space, that could point to a few things: Qwen's embeddings might naturally capture depth patterns that Qwen itself produces, the text quality from Qwen could preserve more nuanced depth gradients, or there's just better architectural alignment between Qwen's embeddings and Qwen-generated text. This is actually a meaningful finding about how well different models' outputs align with a particular embedding space.

The key insight is that K*_neural isn't purely about the text itself—it depends on which embedding model you're using. If Qwen gives K*_neural=3 while Pythia and OLMo give 2, that's evidence of self-familiarity in the embedding model, which is different from the model-free statistical features that stay consistent at K*=2 across all generators. For the paper, this means the neural boundary has a representation-dependent component that the hand-crafted features don't have.

On the interpretability-discriminability tradeoff: this actually seems like a solid Discussion point. The 15D features are interpretable and transferable (K*=2, surprisal variance dominates), while the neural probe is more discriminative but less interpretable and model-dependent (K*=3). Rather than framing this as a limitation, I can present it as a genuine contribution—showing that the depth boundary isn't fixed but shifts depending on your measurement approach, similar to resolution limits in microscopy. This should go into Discussion §5.1 as a subsection on theoretical implications, extending the K*_empirical discussion rather than standing alone.

For the framing, I'd introduce K*_neural=3 by emphasizing that the gap between K*_15D=2 and K*_neural=3 reveals representation-dependence: the 15D features establish an operational lower bound while richer neural representations can extract additional generational signal. This transforms the reviewer concern about not using learned representations from a weak "we used it as a baseline" into a strong empirical finding about how representation choice affects the discrimination boundary.

**cross-model generalizability requires model-free features**\" — 这反而**加强 15D 特征的定位** |\n| 2/3 或混合结果 | 部分 model-dependent | 按数据如实报告，强调 K*_15D=2 的稳定性作为 canonical result |\n\n**关键洞察**：即使 Pythia/OLMo 给 K*_neural=2（最差情况），论文叙事也不受损——反而证明 \"15D features 虽然 K* 更低，但优势在于 model-free transferability，这是 neural probe 不具备的\"。这完美回应了 Reviewer Concern #5（self-familiarity）。\n\n### Q3: 叙事空间 — 建议作为 Discussion 独立子节\n\n[severity: info] **\"interpretability–discriminability tradeoff\" 足以成为 Discussion 的独立亮点（§5.x），不仅是 Limitation 补充。**\n\n论证：\n- 15D features：K*=2，可解释（surprisal variance 主导），model-free，8 条件 universal，可部署\n- Neural probe：K*=3，更高辨别力，但 model-dependent，不可解释，计算成本高\n- 这揭示了一个类似**显微镜分辨率极限**的关系：测量工具（特征表示）的分辨力决定了可观测的边界\n\n建议 Discussion 结构：\n- §5.1 现有内容（K*=2 theoretical implications）保留，但在末尾加一段引出 K*_neural\n- §5.1bis（新）：\"Representation Dependence of K*\" — 对比 15D vs neural probe，阐述 tradeoff，连接到 DPI 理论（K*_theory ≥ K*_neural ≥ K*_15D）\n- 这比放在 Limitation 更好，因为它是一个**积极发现**（我们证明了 K* 的层次结构），不是缺陷\n\n### Q4: paper_framing.md Reviewer Concern #6 — 必须更新\n\n[severity: warning] **当前回答 \"作为 baseline 对比\" 在有 K*_neural=3 数据的情况下过于单薄。**\n\n建议更新 Reviewer Concern #6 回应为：\n\n> \"We explicitly compare 15D hand-crafted features (K*=2) against neural probe representations using frozen LLM embeddings (K*=3). The comparison reveals a representation-dependent hierarchy: K*_theory ≥ K*_neural ≥ K*_15D. Hand-crafted features offer model-free transferability (80-92% cross-model retention), interpretability (feature degradation analysis), and practical deployability. Neural representations achieve higher discriminability (+1 depth level) but at the cost of model dependence and ~100x computational overhead. This tradeoff motivates the 15D feature set as the practical choice while demonstrating that the theoretical boundary extends beyond 2.\"\n\n这将 Concern #6 从防御性回答（\"我们没做\"）转为**主动贡献**（\"我们做了，发现了有趣的 tradeoff\"）。\n\n### 总结\n\nK*_neural=3 对论文是**净正面**，前提是：\n1. 不急于将 headline 从 K*=2 改为 K*=3（等 3 数据集全部确认 + 统计检验）\n2. 将其定位为\"深化 K* characterization\"而非\"推翻 K*=2\"\n3. 利用 interpretability–discriminability tradeoff 填充 Discussion，同时加强 Concern #6 回应\n\n**最大风险**：如果 Pythia/OLMo 重跑也给 K*_neural=3，但论文仍以 K*_15D=2 为 headline，审稿人可能问 \"why bury the stronger result?\" → 需在 framing 中预先解释：K*_15D=2 是 practical/deployable boundary，K*_neural=3 是 theoretical exploration，两者服务不同目的。",
